src/forgex/cli/commands/build.py ✚

python

"""`forgex build` — the full FORGEX workflow (spec sections 12, 22, 34, 35, 57, 58)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Annotated, Optional

import typer
from rich.markup import escape
from rich.panel import Panel

from forgex.cli.ui import JsonFlag, Output, PlainFlag, make_approval, ui_from
from forgex.config import load_config
from forgex.orchestration.engine import BuildEngine, BuildRunResult, DryRunReport
from forgex.orchestration.stores import open_stores
from forgex.schemas.error import ProjectError
from forgex.utils.paths import find_project_root


def _tests_line(result: BuildRunResult) -> str:
    tests = result.tests
    if tests is None:
        return "not run"
    if tests.passed is None:
        return f"exit {tests.exit_code}"
    return f"{tests.passed} passed, {tests.failed or 0} failed"


def _render_success(ui, result: BuildRunResult) -> None:
    if ui.mode is Output.RICH:
        ui.console.print(Panel("[bold green]FORGEX BUILD COMPLETE[/bold green]",
                               expand=False))
    lines = [
        f"Tasks       {result.tasks_completed}/{result.tasks_total}",
        f"Files       {len(result.changed_files)} changed",
        f"Tests       {_tests_line(result)}",
        f"Build       {'passed' if result.build_ok else 'failed' if result.build_ok is False else '—'}",
        f"Supervisor  {result.verdict}",
        f"Iterations  {result.iterations}",
        f"Checkpoint  {result.checkpoint[:8] if result.checkpoint else '—'}",
    ]
    for line in lines:
        ui.text(line)
    if result.changed_files and ui.mode in (Output.RICH, Output.PLAIN):
        ui.text("")
        ui.text("Changed files:")
        for path in result.changed_files[:30]:
            ui.text(f"  {escape(path)}")
    for warning in result.warnings:
    raise typer.Exit(code=0 if result.status == "completed" else 1)

tests/unit/test_cli_build.py ✚

python

import json
import subprocess
from pathlib import Path

from typer.testing import CliRunner

from forgex.cli.app import app
from forgex.orchestration.state import StateStore
from forgex.orchestration.stores import open_stores
from forgex.schemas.plan import ArchitecturePlan, specs_to_tasks

runner = CliRunner()


def test_cli_build_end_to_end(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM", "1")
    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path / "home"))
    (tmp_path / "home").mkdir()
    monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)
    runner.invoke(app, ["init"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q"], cwd=tmp_path, check=True, capture_output=True)
    (tmp_path / "pyproject.toml").write_text(
        '[project]\nname = "demo"\ndependencies = ["pytest"]\n', encoding="utf-8")
    (tmp_path / "test_x.py").write_text("def test_x():\n    assert True\n",
                                        encoding="utf-8")
    plan = ArchitecturePlan.model_validate({
        "goal": "demo",
        "tasks": [{"id": "TASK-001", "description": "First"},
                  {"id": "TASK-002", "description": "Second"}]})
    with open_stores(tmp_path) as stores:
        state = stores.state.load()
        state.user_request = "demo build"
        state.architecture_plan = plan
        stores.save_state(state)
        stores.save_tasks(specs_to_tasks(plan.tasks), project_id=state.project_id)

    # fake the four agent factories inside the engine module
    from tests.unit.test_engine import FakeBuilder, FakeCoder, FakePlanner, FakeSupervisor

    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_planner",
                        lambda **kw: FakePlanner())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_builder",
                        lambda **kw: FakeBuilder())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_coder",
                        lambda **kw: FakeCoder())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_supervisor",
                        lambda **kw: FakeSupervisor())

    result = runner.invoke(app, ["build", "--json"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.output
    payload = json.loads(result.output)
    assert payload["status"] == "completed"
    assert payload["tasks_completed"] == 2
    assert payload["verdict"] == "pass"
    assert StateStore(tmp_path).load().status.value == "completed"


def test_cli_build_requires_request_on_empty_project(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)
    runner.invoke(app, ["init"])
    result = runner.invoke(app, ["build"])
    assert result.exit_code == 4  # ProjectError: provide a request

KNOWN ISSUES:

Live forgex build unverified — everything until now has been mock-verified. The §76 acceptance run above is the gate; expect the loop to teach us things (tool-call formatting, plan quality, repair behavior) that need prompt/loop tuning.
needs_review stops the run rather than repairing (repair can't fix "cannot verify") — by design, reported honestly.
Failed builder tasks are not re-attempted by the Builder across iterations (§12 routes repair through the Expert Coder); a builder-retry refinement is a Phase 13 candidate.

"""`forgex build` — the full FORGEX workflow (spec sections 12, 22, 34, 35, 57, 58)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Annotated, Optional

import typer
from rich.markup import escape
from rich.panel import Panel

from forgex.cli.ui import JsonFlag, Output, PlainFlag, make_approval, ui_from
from forgex.config import load_config
from forgex.orchestration.engine import BuildEngine, BuildRunResult, DryRunReport
from forgex.orchestration.stores import open_stores
from forgex.schemas.error import ProjectError
from forgex.utils.paths import find_project_root


def _tests_line(result: BuildRunResult) -> str:
    tests = result.tests
    if tests is None:
        return "not run"
    if tests.passed is None:
        return f"exit {tests.exit_code}"
    return f"{tests.passed} passed, {tests.failed or 0} failed"


def _render_success(ui, result: BuildRunResult) -> None:
    if ui.mode is Output.RICH:
        ui.console.print(Panel("[bold green]FORGEX BUILD COMPLETE[/bold green]",
                               expand=False))
    lines = [
        f"Tasks       {result.tasks_completed}/{result.tasks_total}",
        f"Files       {len(result.changed_files)} changed",
        f"Tests       {_tests_line(result)}",
        f"Build       {'passed' if result.build_ok else 'failed' if result.build_ok is False else '—'}",
        f"Supervisor  {result.verdict}",
        f"Iterations  {result.iterations}",
        f"Checkpoint  {result.checkpoint[:8] if result.checkpoint else '—'}",
    ]
    for line in lines:
        ui.text(line)
    if result.changed_files and ui.mode in (Output.RICH, Output.PLAIN):
        ui.text("")
        ui.text("Changed files:")
        for path in result.changed_files[:30]:
            ui.text(f"  {escape(path)}")
    for warning in result.warnings:
    raise typer.Exit(code=0 if result.status == "completed" else 1)

tests/unit/test_engine.py ✚

python

from forgex.schemas.task import TaskStatus

PLAN = [
    {"id": "TASK-001", "description": "First"},
    {"id": "TASK-002", "description": "Second", "depends_on": ["TASK-001"]},
]
PASSING_TEST = "def test_x():\n    assert True\n"
FAILING_TEST = "def test_x():\n    assert False\n"


class FakePlanner:
    async def plan(self, request, *, on_stage=None):
        return ArchitectResult(
            plan=ArchitecturePlan.model_validate(
                {"goal": "x", "tasks": [{"id": "TASK-001", "description": "only"}]})
        )

    async def aclose(self):
        return None


class FakeBuilder:
    def __init__(self, fail=(), interrupt_on=None):
        self.fail = set(fail)
        self.interrupt_on = interrupt_on
        self.ran: list[str] = []

    async def execute(self, task, *, plan, research=None):
        self.ran.append(task.id)
        if self.interrupt_on == task.id:
            raise KeyboardInterrupt
        if task.id in self.fail:
            return BuilderResult(status="failed", summary=f"cannot build {task.id}",
                                 issues=["stubborn failure"])
        return BuilderResult(status="completed", summary=f"built {task.id}",
                             changed_files=[f"out_{task.id}.txt"])

    async def aclose(self):
        return None


class FakeCoder:
    def __init__(self, fix=None):
        self.fix = fix
        self.calls: list[str | None] = []

    async def review(self, *, task=None, plan=None, research=None, diff="",
                     changed_files=None, errors=None, extra_context=""):
        self.calls.append(task.id if task else None)
        if self.fix is not None and task and task.id.startswith("REPAIR-"):
            self.fix()
            return ExpertCoderResult(status="completed", summary="repaired",
                                     changed_files=["test_x.py"],
                                     improvements=["fixed the failing test"])
        return ExpertCoderResult(status="completed", summary="reviewed; clean")

    async def aclose(self):
        return None


class FakeSupervisor:
    def __init__(self, verdict="pass"):
        self.verdict = verdict
        self.calls = 0

    async def audit(self, **kwargs):
        self.calls += 1
        return SupervisorJudgment(status=self.verdict, summary="checked")

    async def aclose(self):
        return None


def seed(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch, *, plan_tasks=PLAN,
         test_body=PASSING_TEST) -> None:
    monkeypatch.setenv("GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM", "1")
    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path / "home"))
    (tmp_path / "home").mkdir()
    assert len(TaskStore(tmp_path).load()) == 2  # untouched

runner = CliRunner()


def test_cli_build_end_to_end(tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setenv("GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM", "1")
    monkeypatch.setenv("HOME", str(tmp_path / "home"))
    (tmp_path / "home").mkdir()
    monkeypatch.chdir(tmp_path)
    runner.invoke(app, ["init"])
    subprocess.run(["git", "init", "-q"], cwd=tmp_path, check=True, capture_output=True)
    (tmp_path / "pyproject.toml").write_text(
        '[project]\nname = "demo"\ndependencies = ["pytest"]\n', encoding="utf-8")
    (tmp_path / "test_x.py").write_text("def test_x():\n    assert True\n",
                                        encoding="utf-8")
    plan = ArchitecturePlan.model_validate({
        "goal": "demo",
        "tasks": [{"id": "TASK-001", "description": "First"},
                  {"id": "TASK-002", "description": "Second"}]})
    with open_stores(tmp_path) as stores:
        state = stores.state.load()
        state.user_request = "demo build"
        state.architecture_plan = plan
        stores.save_state(state)
        stores.save_tasks(specs_to_tasks(plan.tasks), project_id=state.project_id)

    # fake the four agent factories inside the engine module
    from tests.unit.test_engine import FakeBuilder, FakeCoder, FakePlanner, FakeSupervisor

    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_planner",
                        lambda **kw: FakePlanner())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_builder",
                        lambda **kw: FakeBuilder())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_coder",
                        lambda **kw: FakeCoder())
    monkeypatch.setattr("forgex.orchestration.engine.build_supervisor",
                        lambda **kw: FakeSupervisor())

    result = runner.invoke(app, ["build", "--json"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.output
    payload = json.loads(result.output)
    assert payload["status"] == "completed"
    assert payload["tasks_completed"] == 2
    assert payload["verdict"] == "pass"
    assert StateStore(tmp_path).load().status.value == "completed"

phase 10 

Thought Process
Phase 10 — the Docker sandbox. This closes the biggest honest gap from Phase 4: policy was command-level, and python -c could still do arbitrary compute on the host. After this phase, when sandbox.enabled=true, every FORGEX-executed command runs inside a hardened container where the host filesystem simply doesn't exist outside the workspace mount.

(One-line nag, then I'll drop it: I still haven't seen your pytest -q or the Phase 9 live forgex build output. The sandbox phase makes the live run safer — so ideally: run the suite, then do the §76 acceptance test with sandbox on.)

PHASE 10 — DOCKER SANDBOX
10.0 Design decisions (ADR additions)
Refactor: policy gating moves into a shared base. PolicyGatedExecutor now owns argv normalization + command policy + approval + cwd confinement; RestrictedLocalExecutor and the new DockerExecutor implement only _execute(). Same public CommandExecutor interface, same CommandResult — zero changes for callers; all Phase 4 executor tests must keep passing unchanged.
Two-layer network control (§20): layer 1 = the command policy (approval for pip/npm/… since Phase 4); layer 2 = --network none on the container by default. Approving a network command is necessary but not sufficient — the sandbox must also have sandbox.network=true. Restricted by default, exactly as specified.
Hardened container flags by default: --rm, --cap-drop ALL, --security-opt no-new-privileges, --read-only rootfs + tmpfs /tmp, --network none, --user <uid>:<gid> (POSIX — files written in the workspace are owned by you, not root), workspace mounted RW at /workspace (the only host path that exists), -w /workspace/<cwd>. Resource limits (--cpus, --memory) from config.
Honest degradation, logged: sandbox.enabled=true + Docker missing → warning + restricted local executor. Never a silent downgrade. Docker daemon errors surface as real command results (docker exit 125 + stderr) — the agent sees the truth.
No hidden image pulls. If python:3.12-slim (configurable) isn't present, doctor tells you to pull it — pulling is a network operation we won't perform silently.
Timeout/kill correctness: each container gets a unique --name; on timeout or Ctrl+C we docker kill the container then kill the client (§36 — no orphaned containers thanks to --rm).
Selection: build_executor() (new factory) chooses Docker vs local; create_context() uses it — so run_command, run_tests, the verification harness, and the whole Phase 9 engine become sandboxed with zero changes to any of them.

src/forgex/config/schema.py ✏ — replace SandboxConfig:

python

class SandboxConfig(BaseModel):
    """Docker sandbox (spec section 20). Default: restricted.

    enabled=false → restricted local executor (Phase 4 guarantees).
    enabled=true  → every command runs in a hardened container:
    workspace-only mount, no network unless network=true, dropped
    capabilities, read-only rootfs, resource limits. Requires the image
    to be present (forgex doctor checks; we never auto-pull)."""

    enabled: bool = False
    network: bool = False
    image: str = "python:3.12-slim"
    cpu_limit: str | None = None  # docker --cpus, e.g. "2.0"
    memory_limit: str | None = None  # docker --memory, e.g. "1g"
    read_only: bool = True  # read-only container rootfs + tmpfs /tmp
    user: str | None = None  # explicit --user; default: host uid:gid on POSIX
    timeout_seconds: int = 120

src/forgex/sandbox/executor.py ✏ (full replacement)

python

    config,  # ForgexConfig
    approval: ApprovalCallback | None = None,
) -> CommandExecutor:
    """Select the executor: Docker when enabled AND installed, else the
    restricted local executor. Downgrades are always logged, never silent."""
    policy = CommandPolicy.from_config(config)
    if config.sandbox.enabled:
        if docker_available():
            sandbox = config.sandbox
            logger.info(
                "sandbox: Docker executor (image=%s, network=%s, cpus=%s, memory=%s)",
                sandbox.image, "on" if sandbox.network else "off",
                sandbox.cpu_limit or "unlimited", sandbox.memory_limit or "unlimited",
            )
            return DockerExecutor(
                workspace=workspace, policy=policy, approval=approval,
                image=sandbox.image, cpu_limit=sandbox.cpu_limit,
                memory_limit=sandbox.memory_limit, network=sandbox.network,
                read_only=sandbox.read_only, user=sandbox.user,
                default_timeout=sandbox.timeout_seconds,
                max_output_bytes=config.terminal.max_output_bytes,
            )
        logger.warning(
            "sandbox.enabled is true but docker is not installed — "
            "using the restricted LOCAL executor (logged, not silent)"
        )
    return RestrictedLocalExecutor(
        workspace=workspace, policy=policy, approval=approval,
        default_timeout=config.terminal.timeout_seconds,
        max_output_bytes=config.terminal.max_output_bytes,
    )

src/forgex/cli/commands/doctor.py ✏ — make the Docker check sandbox-aware. Add import subprocess to the imports, add this helper, and replace the _check_binary("docker", ...) line in the checks list with *_check_docker(config):

python

def _check_docker(config) -> list[Check]:
    """Docker check: binary → (required when sandbox enabled) → image presence."""
    found = shutil.which("docker") is not None
    sandbox_on = config is not None and config.sandbox.enabled
    purpose = "sandboxed command execution" + (" (ENABLED)" if sandbox_on else " (disabled)")
    checks = [Check("Docker", found, required=sandbox_on,
                    detail=f"found — {purpose}" if found else f"not found — {purpose}")]
    if sandbox_on and found:
        image = config.sandbox.image
        try:
            proc = subprocess.run(["docker", "image", "inspect", image],
                                  capture_output=True, timeout=15, check=False)
            ok = proc.returncode == 0
            detail = (f"image {image} present" if ok
                      else f"image missing — run: docker pull {image}")
        except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
            ok, detail = False, "docker daemon unreachable"
        checks.append(Check("Sandbox image", ok, required=False, detail=detail))
    return checks
Docs
docs/sandbox.md ✚

Sandbox
When sandbox.enabled = true and Docker is installed, every command FORGEXexecutes (run_command, run_tests, the verification harness — everything)runs inside a hardened container. The Phase 4 restricted local executor isthe always-available fallback (logged, never silent).

Enable
# ~/.forgex/config.toml (or project .forgex/config.toml)[sandbox]enabled = trueimage = "python:3.12-slim"   # must be present: docker pull python:3.12-slim# network = false             # container gets --network none (default)# cpu_limit = "2.0"           # docker --cpus# memory_limit = "1g"         # docker --memory# read_only = true            # read-only container rootfs + tmpfs /tmp# user = "1000:1000"          # default: host uid:gid (POSIX)
Check readiness with forgex doctor (it verifies the image).

Guarantees
Control
Implementation
Host filesystem invisible	only -v <workspace>:/workspace is mounted
No network by default	--network none; set sandbox.network=true to allow
Least privilege	--cap-drop ALL, --security-opt no-new-privileges
Read-only system	--read-only rootfs + tmpfs /tmp
Resource limits	--cpus / --memory when configured
Your files stay yours	--user <uid>:<gid> on POSIX (no root-owned files)
No orphans	--rm + named containers killed on timeout/interrupt
Command policy	unchanged (Phase 4): denied list, allowlist, network approval

Two-layer network control
Approving a network command (e.g. pip install) at the policy layer is
necessary but not sufficient: the container also needs sandbox.network=true.
With network off, approved installs fail with network errors inside the
container — that is the restricted default working as designed.

Notes & limits
The image is never auto-pulled (pulling is a network operation we don't hide).
Docker exit 125 = daemon error, 126/127 = command problems in the container;
errors are passed through verbatim to the agent.
Windows: Docker Desktop handles uid mapping; mounts use forward-slash paths.
read_only = false is the escape hatch if an exotic toolchain must write
outside the workspace/tmp.
text

    with pytest.raises(CommandDeniedError):
        await executor.run(["rm", "-rf", "x"])


async def test_cwd_confinement_shared(tmp_path):
    class NeverReached(PolicyGatedExecutor):
        async def _execute(self, argv, workdir, timeout, env):  # pragma: no cover
            raise AssertionError("unreachable")

    from forgex.schemas.error import SandboxError

    executor = NeverReached(workspace=tmp_path, policy=CommandPolicy())
    with pytest.raises(SandboxError, match="escapes workspace"):
        await executor.run([sys.executable, "-V"], cwd="../outside")


# ---- live Docker e2e (skipped unless docker + image are present) -----------

def _docker_ready() -> bool:
    if not shutil.which("docker"):
        return False
    try:
        return subprocess.run(
            ["docker", "image", "inspect", DockerExecutor.DEFAULT_IMAGE],
            capture_output=True, timeout=15, check=False,
        ).returncode == 0
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
        return False


pytestmark_docker = pytest.mark.skipif(not _docker_ready(), reason="docker/image unavailable")


@pytest.mark.asyncio
@pytestmark_docker
async def test_docker_executor_runs_python(tmp_path):
    executor = make_docker(tmp_path)
    result = await executor.run(["python", "-c", "print('sandboxed')"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.stderr
    assert "sandboxed" in result.stdout


@pytest.mark.asyncio
@pytestmark_docker
async def test_docker_workspace_isolation(tmp_path):
    executor = make_docker(tmp_path)
    result = await executor.run(
        ["python", "-c", "open('sandbox_marker.txt', 'w').write('hi')"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0, result.stderr
    assert (tmp_path / "sandbox_marker.txt").read_text(encoding="utf-8") == "hi"


@pytest.mark.asyncio
@pytestmark_docker
async def test_docker_network_disabled_by_default(tmp_path):
    executor = make_docker(tmp_path, network=False)
    result = await executor.run(
        ["python", "-c",
         "import urllib.request; urllib.request.urlopen('https://pypi.org', timeout=5)"])
    assert result.exit_code != 0  # --network none: no way out

KNOWN ISSUES:

The 3 Docker e2e tests are skipped unless docker + image are present — the offline argv tests prove flag construction, not that your Docker daemon accepts them. The manual walkthrough above is the real verification.
No auto-pull of the image (deliberate); first-time users must docker pull — doctor tells them.
Docker exit 125/126/127 pass through raw — an agent sees docker daemon errors as command failures (honest, but a friendlier wrapper is a Phase 13 polish item).
Windows mounts rely on Docker Desktop's path handling (forward slashes); Git-Bash MSYS path mangling is a user-env issue.
--read-only may break toolchains that write outside the workspace/tmp — sandbox.read_only=false is the documented escape hatch.
Image is assumed to contain the project's runtime (default python:3.12-slim) — a JS/Rust project needs a matching sandbox.image; per-ecosystem image defaults are a Phase 13 nicety.
Container startup adds ~0.3–1s per command — noticeable in the verification harness; a persistent-container mode is a future optimization.

phase 11 

Thought Process
Phase 11 — Context Intelligence. This is where FORGEX stops being token-blind (§16): a cached project index (files, symbols, imports — AST-based for Python), an import dependency graph, lexical relevance scoring with transparent reasons, chunk-based context compression, and fail-open wiring into the Builder (related files), Expert Coder (impact analysis — files that import what changed), and Architect (repo symbol map). No vector DB — §17 explicitly says not required for MVP, and lexical + import-graph signals are honest and testable.

(Integration is strictly additive and fail-open — context intelligence must never block a build — so all existing agent tests keep passing.)

PHASE 11 — CONTEXT INTELLIGENCE
11.0 Design decisions (ADR additions)
Index = facts, cached. context/indexer.py walks the project (ignore-dirs + secret-file rules reused from the tools layer) and records per file: size, mtime, language, top-level symbols (AST for Python; regex-heuristic for JS/TS, labeled as such), imports. Cache at .forgex/cache/context-index.json (already gitignored), invalidated per-file by size+mtime — re-parse only what changed, prune deleted files. Stats (parsed/reused/pruned) are recorded for observability.
Dependency graph from imports, path-resolution heuristic. Python: relative imports resolved against the importer's package; absolute imports matched to project paths (mod.sub → mod/sub.py or mod/sub/__init__.py, shortest-path wins on ambiguity). JS/TS: relative specifiers resolved with extension/index candidates; bare specifiers are external → ignored. Go/Rust imports not parsed yet (honest gap).
Relevance scoring with reasons (§16 file relevance scoring). Signals: seed (+100), direct importer/importee (+40), transitive depth-2 (+15), related test file (+30), recently-changed (+20), symbol-name match (+10), path match (+5). Every contribution is recorded as a human-readable reason — selection is auditable, not a black box.
Compression by chunking (§16): oversized files are reduced to the module head (imports always matter) + matched chunks (Python: AST def/class blocks with line markers) + filler within budget, with explicit elision markers — never silent truncation of structure.
Fail-open integration: Builder/Expert-Coder/Architect wrap context calls in try/except-with-warning; on any failure they proceed exactly as before. Builder gets related files beyond planned ones; the Expert Coder gets importers of changed files (downstream impact — the highest-value signal for review); the Architect's repo brief gains a compact symbol map.
New CLI: forgex context stats|search — the debug surface for trusting selection.

"""Context intelligence (spec sections 16, 17).

Targeted context: cached project index (files, symbols, imports),
import dependency graph, lexical relevance scoring with auditable
reasons, and chunk-based compression. No vector database required."""

from forgex.context.manager import ContextBundle, ContextManager

__all__ = ["ContextBundle", "ContextManager", "ScoredFile", "query_terms"]
src/forgex/context/indexer.py ✚

python

            symbols.append(SymbolInfo(name=node.name, kind="class", line=node.lineno))
            for child in node.body:
                if isinstance(child, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef)):
                    symbols.append(
                        SymbolInfo(name=f"{node.name}.{child.name}", kind="method",
                                   line=child.lineno))
    for node in ast.walk(tree):
        if isinstance(node, ast.Import):
            imports.extend(alias.name for alias in node.names)
        elif isinstance(node, ast.ImportFrom):
            prefix = "." * node.level
            imports.append(prefix + (node.module or ""))
    return symbols[:MAX_SYMBOLS_PER_FILE], imports[:MAX_IMPORTS_PER_FILE]


_JS_IMPORT_RE = re.compile(
    r"""(?:import\s+[^;{}]*?from\s*|import\s*|export\s+[^;{}]*?from\s*|require\(\s*)['"]([^'"]+)['"]"""
)
_JS_SYMBOL_RE = re.compile(
    r"export\s+(?:default\s+)?(?:async\s+)?(?:function|class|const|let)\s+(\w+)"
)


def _js_parse(source: str) -> tuple[list[SymbolInfo], list[str]]:
    imports = _JS_IMPORT_RE.findall(source)[:MAX_IMPORTS_PER_FILE]
    symbols = [SymbolInfo(name=name, kind="export", line=0)
               for name in _JS_SYMBOL_RE.findall(source)[:MAX_SYMBOLS_PER_FILE]]
    return symbols, imports


def index_file(path: Path, rel: str, size: int, mtime: float) -> FileIndex:
    language = LANGUAGE_BY_SUFFIX.get(path.suffix.lower(), "other")
    entry = FileIndex(size=size, mtime=mtime, language=language)
    if language in PARSEABLE and size <= MAX_PARSE_BYTES:
        try:
            source = path.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
        except OSError:
            return entry
        symbols, imports = (_python_parse(source) if language == "python"
                            else _js_parse(source))
        entry.symbols, entry.imports = symbols, imports
    return entry


def build(workspace: Path, cached: IndexData | None = None) -> IndexData:
    """Build the index, reusing cached entries whose size+mtime are unchanged.
    Deleted files are pruned; stats record the work done."""
    workspace = workspace.resolve()
    cached_files = cached.files if cached and cached.version == INDEX_VERSION else {}
    files: dict[str, FileIndex] = {}
    parsed = reused = 0
    for path in iter_project_files(workspace):
        rel = path.relative_to(workspace).as_posix()
        try:
            stat = path.stat()
        except OSError:
            continue
        old = cached_files.get(rel)
        if old is not None and old.size == stat.st_size and old.mtime == stat.st_mtime:
            files[rel] = old
            reused += 1
            continue
        files[rel] = index_file(path, rel, stat.st_size, stat.st_mtime)
        parsed += 1
    pruned = len(cached_files) - reused
    return IndexData(
        generated_at=datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(), files=files,
        stats={"parsed": parsed, "reused": reused, "pruned": pruned},
    )
src/forgex/context/dependency_graph.py ✚

"""Import dependency graph (spec section 17).

Best-effort resolution of imports to project files:
- Python: relative imports resolved against the importer's package;
  absolute imports matched by module path (a.b -> a/b.py or a/b/__init__.py),
  shortest path wins on ambiguity.
- JS/TS: relative specifiers resolved with extension/index candidates;
  bare specifiers are external packages -> ignored.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import posixpath
from collections import deque

from forgex.context.indexer import IndexData


def _resolve_python(imp: str, files: dict) -> str | None:
    if imp.startswith("."):
        level = len(imp) - len(imp.lstrip("."))
        base = imp.lstrip(".").replace(".", "/")
        return _match(files, [base + ".py", base + "/__init__.py",
                              "__init__.py" if not base else base])
    base = imp.replace(".", "/")
    candidates = [base + ".py", base + "/__init__.py"]
src/forgex/context/chunker.py ✚

python

"""Chunking + selection (spec section 16: context compression).

Oversized files are reduced to: module head (imports always matter) +
chunks matching the query terms + filler — with explicit elision markers.
Python chunks come from AST def/class blocks; other files use line windows.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import ast
import re
from dataclasses import dataclass

_CAMEL_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Z]?[a-z]+|[A-Z]+(?![a-z])")


@dataclass
class Chunk:
    name: str
    text: str


def chunk_python(source: str) -> list[Chunk]:
    chunks: list[Chunk] = []
    try:
        tree = ast.parse(source)
    except SyntaxError:
        return [Chunk("whole-file", source)]

    def node_end(node) -> int:
        return getattr(node, "end_lineno", None) or node.lineno

    first_line = min((n.lineno for n in tree.body), default=len(source.splitlines()) + 1)
    head_lines = source.splitlines()[: max(first_line - 1, 0)]
    if any(line.strip() for line in head_lines):
        chunks.append(Chunk("module-head", "\n".join(head_lines)))

    for node in tree.body:
        if isinstance(node, (ast.FunctionDef, ast.AsyncFunctionDef, ast.ClassDef)):
            lines = source.splitlines()
            body = "\n".join(lines[node.lineno - 1: node_end(node)])
            kind = "class" if isinstance(node, ast.ClassDef) else "def"
            chunks.append(Chunk(node.name, f"# --- {kind} {node.name} (line {node.lineno}) ---\n{body}"))
    return chunks


def chunk_generic(source: str, window: int = 80) -> list[Chunk]:
    lines = source.splitlines()
    if len(lines) <= window:
        return [Chunk("whole-file", source)]
    return [
        Chunk(f"lines-{i + 1}-{min(i + window, len(lines))}",
              f"# --- lines {i + 1}-{min(i + window, len(lines))} ---\n"
              + "\n".join(lines[i:i + window]))
        for i in range(0, len(lines), window)
    ]


def _tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    out: set[str] = set()
    for part in re.split(r"[^A-Za-z0-9]+", text.lower()):
        out.update(p for p in part.split("_") if len(p) >= 3)
    for token in re.findall(r"[A-Za-z]{3,}", text):
        out.update(p.lower() for p in _CAMEL_RE.findall(token))
    return out


def _matches(chunk: Chunk, terms: set[str]) -> bool:
    chunk_terms = _tokens(chunk.name) | _tokens(chunk.text[:2000])
    return bool(chunk_terms & terms)


def select_chunks(chunks: list[Chunk], terms: set[str], max_chars: int) -> tuple[str, bool]:
    """Assemble chunks within max_chars: head first, then term matches,
    then filler. Returns (text, truncated)."""
    total = sum(len(chunk.text) for chunk in chunks)
    if total <= max_chars:
        return "\n\n".join(chunk.text for chunk in chunks), False

    head = next((c for c in chunks if c.name == "module-head"), None)
    ordered: list[Chunk] = []
    if head is not None:
        ordered.append(head)
    rest = [c for c in chunks if c is not head]
    ordered.extend(c for c in rest if _matches(c, terms))
    ordered.extend(c for c in rest if not _matches(c, terms))

    parts: list[str] = []
    used = 0
    for chunk in ordered:
        if used >= max_chars:
            break
        text = chunk.text
        if len(text) > max_chars - used:
            text = text[: max_chars - used] + "\n… [chunk trimmed]"
        parts.append(text)
        used += len(text)
    return "\n… [lines omitted] …\n".join(parts), True
src/forgex/context/retriever.py ✚

python

"""Lexical relevance retrieval with auditable reasons (spec section 16)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from forgex.context.dependency_graph import DependencyGraph
from forgex.context.indexer import IndexData

STOPWORDS = {
    "the", "and", "for", "with", "into", "from", "this", "that", "must",
    "should", "will", "your", "are", "all", "not", "but", "can", "has",
    "have", "when", "which", "was", "you", "its", "our",
}
_CAMEL_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Z]?[a-z]+|[A-Z]+(?![a-z])")


def query_terms(text: str, *, limit: int = 12) -> list[str]:
    terms: list[str] = []
    for token in re.findall(r"[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]{2,}", text):
        for part in _CAMEL_RE.findall(token.replace("_", " ")) or [token]:
            p = part.lower()
            if len(p) >= 3 and p not in STOPWORDS and p not in terms:
                terms.append(p)
        snake = token.lower().replace("_", " ").split()
        for p in snake:
            if len(p) >= 3 and p not in STOPWORDS and p not in terms:
                terms.append(p)
    return terms[:limit]


@dataclass
class ScoredFile:
    path: str
    score: int = 0
    reasons: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)

    def bump(self, points: int, reason: str) -> None:
        self.score += points
        self.reasons.append(reason)


def _path_tokens(path: str) -> set[str]:
    return {t for t in re.split(r"[^a-z0-9]+", path.lower()) if len(t) >= 3}


def _is_test_path(path: str) -> bool:
    return "test" in path.lower()


def score_files(
    *,
    index: IndexData,
    graph: DependencyGraph,
    query: str,
    seeds: list[str],
    recent: list[str] | None = None,
    limit: int = 20,
) -> list[ScoredFile]:
    """Rank project files for a query. Seeds and their dependency
    neighborhoods dominate; lexical matches refine. Every point is reasoned."""
    scores: dict[str, ScoredFile] = {}

    def get(path: str) -> ScoredFile:
        return scores.setdefault(path, ScoredFile(path=path))

    terms = set(query_terms(query))

    for seed in seeds:
        if seed in index.files:
            get(seed).bump(100, "seed file (planned/changed)")
        for importer, depth in graph.importers_of(seed, max_depth=2).items():
            if importer in index.files:
                points = 40 if depth == 1 else 15
                get(importer).bump(points, f"imports seed ({'direct' if depth == 1 else 'transitive'})")
        for dependency in graph.imports_of(seed, max_depth=1):
            if dependency in index.files:
                get(dependency).bump(40, "imported by seed")
        seed_tokens = _path_tokens(seed)
        if seed_tokens:
            for path in index.files:
                if _is_test_path(path) and _path_tokens(path) & seed_tokens:
                    get(path).bump(30, f"test file for {seed}")

    for path in recent or []:
        if path in index.files:
            get(path).bump(20, "recently changed")

    for path, entry in index.files.items():
        lowered = path.lower()
        for term in terms:
            if term in lowered:
                get(path).bump(5, f"path matches '{term}'")
            for symbol in entry.symbols:
                if term in symbol.name.lower():
                    get(path).bump(10, f"symbol '{symbol.name}' matches '{term}'")
                    break

    ranked = sorted(scores.values(), key=lambda s: (-s.score, s.path))
    return [s for s in ranked if s.score > 0][:limit]

src/forgex/cli/commands/context.py ✚

python

"""`forgex context` — inspect the context index and retrieval (spec section 17)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Annotated, Optional

import typer
from rich.table import Table

from forgex.cli.ui import JsonFlag, Output, PlainFlag, ui_from
from forgex.context.manager import ContextManager
from forgex.schemas.error import ProjectError
from forgex.utils.paths import find_project_root

app = typer.Typer(no_args_is_help=True, help="Inspect the project context index.")


@app.command("stats")
def stats_command(
    ctx: typer.Context,
    refresh: Annotated[bool, typer.Option("--refresh", help="Force a full re-index.")] = False,
    json_out: JsonFlag = False,
    plain: PlainFlag = False,
) -> None:
    """Index summary: files, languages, symbols, cache stats."""
    ui = ui_from(ctx, json_out=json_out, plain=plain)
    root = find_project_root()
    if root is None:
        raise ProjectError("Not inside a FORGEX project — run `forgex init` first.")
    index = ContextManager(root).ensure_index(refresh=refresh)
    languages: dict[str, int] = {}
    symbols = imports = 0
    for entry in index.files.values():
        languages[entry.language] = languages.get(entry.language, 0) + 1
        symbols += len(entry.symbols)

src/forgex/agents/builder.py ✏ — replace _prefetch_existing and add the related-context helper:

python

    def _prefetch_existing(self, task: Task) -> str:
        """Planned files first (always), then related context via the
        ContextManager — token-targeted, never whole-file dumps (spec section 16)."""
        workspace = self._tools.context.workspace
        planned = prefetch_files(workspace, task.files, budget=self._max_prefetch_chars)
        remaining = self._max_prefetch_chars - len(planned)
        if remaining <= 0:
            return planned
        extra = self._related_context(workspace, query=task.description,
                                      seeds=list(task.files), budget=remaining,
                                      exclude=set(task.files))
        return planned + ("\n\n" + extra if extra else "")

    def _related_context(self, workspace, *, query: str, seeds: list[str],
                         budget: int, exclude: set[str]) -> str:
        """Fail-open: context intelligence must never break a build."""
        try:
            from forgex.context.manager import ContextManager

            bundle = ContextManager(workspace).build_context(
                query=query, seeds=seeds, budget=budget, max_files=4,
                exclude=exclude,
            )
            return bundle.render()
        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001
            logger.warning("context retrieval failed; planned files only",
                           exc_info=True)
            return ""

tests/unit/test_indexer.py ✚

python

from pathlib import Path

from forgex.context import indexer
from forgex.context.dependency_graph import DependencyGraph
from forgex.context.indexer import IndexData, build


def write(tmp_path: Path, rel: str, content: str) -> None:
    path = tmp_path / rel
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(content, encoding="utf-8")


def test_python_symbols_and_imports(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/app.py",
          "import os\nfrom . import models\nfrom services import auth\n\n"
          "class UserService:\n    def login(self):\n        pass\n\n"
          "    def logout(self):\n        pass\n\n\ndef helper():\n    pass\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    entry = index.files["src/app.py"]
    names = {s.name for s in entry.symbols}
    assert {"UserService", "UserService.login", "UserService.logout", "helper"} <= names
    assert set(entry.imports) >= {"os", ".models", "services"}


def test_syntax_error_tolerated(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "broken.py", "def f(:\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    assert index.files["broken.py"].symbols == []


def test_js_imports_and_exports(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "app.js",
          "import { tool } from './utils';\nconst x = require('fs');\n"
          "export function run() {}\nexport class Engine {}\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    entry = index.files["app.js"]
    assert "./utils" in entry.imports and "fs" in entry.imports
    assert {"run", "Engine"} <= {s.name for s in entry.symbols}


def test_secret_and_noise_files_skipped(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, ".env", "SECRET=1")
    write(tmp_path, "node_modules/pkg.js", "export const x = 1;")
    write(tmp_path, "real.py", "x = 1\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    assert set(index.files) == {"real.py"}


def test_cache_invalidation_by_size(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "a.py", "def a(): pass\n")
    write(tmp_path, "b.py", "def b(): pass\n")
    first = build(tmp_path)
    assert first.stats["parsed"] == 2

    cached = IndexData.model_validate_json(first.model_dump_json())
    write(tmp_path, "a.py", "def a():\n    return 42\n")  # changed (size differs)
    second = build(tmp_path, cached)
    assert second.stats["parsed"] == 1
    assert second.stats["reused"] == 1
    assert second.files["a.py"].symbols[0].name == "a"


def test_deleted_files_pruned(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "gone.py", "x = 1\n")
    first = build(tmp_path)
    (tmp_path / "gone.py").unlink()
    second = build(tmp_path, first)
    assert "gone.py" not in second.files
    assert second.stats["pruned"] == 1


def test_graph_python_resolution(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/app.py", "from .models import User\nfrom utils.helpers import tool\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/models.py", "class User: pass\n")
    write(tmp_path, "utils/helpers.py", "def tool(): pass\n")
    graph = DependencyGraph(build(tmp_path))
    assert set(graph.imports_of("src/app.py")) == {"src/models.py", "utils/helpers.py"}
    assert graph.importers_of("src/models.py") == {"src/app.py": 1}


def test_graph_transitive_importers(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "a.py", "import b\n")
    write(tmp_path, "b.py", "import c\n")
    write(tmp_path, "c.py", "x = 1\n")
    graph = DependencyGraph(build(tmp_path))
    assert graph.importers_of("c.py", max_depth=2) == {"b.py": 1, "a.py": 2}


def test_graph_js_relative_and_external(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/app.js", "import { t } from './utils';\nimport React from 'react';\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/utils.js", "export const t = 1;\n")
    graph = DependencyGraph(build(tmp_path))
    assert graph.imports_of("src/app.js") == {"src/utils.js": 1}
    assert graph.importers_of("src/utils.js") == {"src/app.js": 1}

from pathlib import Path

def write(tmp_path: Path, rel: str, content: str) -> None:
    path = tmp_path / rel
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(content, encoding="utf-8")


def test_query_terms():
    terms = query_terms("Implement user_service loginHandler for the API")
    assert "user" in terms and "service" in terms and "login" in terms and "handler" in terms
    assert "the" not in terms and "for" not in terms


def test_chunker_selects_matching_symbols():
    source = (
        "import os\n\n"
        "def login():\n    return 'in'\n\n\n"
        "def logout():\n    return 'out'\n"
    )
    chunks = chunk_python(source)
    names = {c.name for c in chunks}
    assert "module-head" in names and "login" in names and "logout" in names
    text, truncated = select_chunks(chunks, {"login"}, max_chars=60)
    assert truncated is True
    assert "import os" in text and "def login" in text


def test_seeds_importers_and_tests_ranked(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/auth.py", "from .models import User\n\n"
                                   "class AuthService:\n    def login(self):\n        pass\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/models.py", "class User:\n    pass\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/app.py", "from .auth import AuthService\n")
    write(tmp_path, "tests/test_auth.py", "from src.auth import AuthService\n")
    write(tmp_path, "docs/readme.md", "# docs\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    graph = DependencyGraph(index)
    ranked = score_files(index=index, graph=graph,
                         query="user login authentication", seeds=["src/auth.py"])
    paths = [s.path for s in ranked]
    assert paths[0] == "src/auth.py"                      # seed
    assert "src/app.py" in paths[:3]                       # importer
    assert "tests/test_auth.py" in paths                   # related test
    seed = ranked[0]
    assert any("seed" in r for r in seed.reasons)
    app = next(s for s in ranked if s.path == "src/app.py")
    assert any("imports seed" in r for r in app.reasons)


def test_symbol_match_without_seeds(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "billing.py", "def process_payment():\n    pass\n")
    write(tmp_path, "other.py", "x = 1\n")
    index = build(tmp_path)
    ranked = score_files(index=index, graph=DependencyGraph(index),
                         query="payment processing", seeds=[])
    assert ranked and ranked[0].path == "billing.py"
    assert any("symbol" in r for r in ranked[0].reasons)

def test_build_context_respects_budget(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/big.py", "import os\n\ndef a():\n    pass\n" + "# padding\n" * 400)
    write(tmp_path, "src/small.py", "def small():\n    pass\n")

                                   budget=2000, max_files=4)

    total = sum(len(f.content) for f in bundle.files)
    assert total <= 2000 + 200  # budget + small slack for markers
    assert bundle.stats["index_files"] == 2

import json
from pathlib import Path

from forgex.agents.builder import BuilderAgent
from forgex.agents.expert_coder import ExpertCoderAgent
from forgex.config import load_config
from forgex.models.base import ModelResponse
from forgex.models.capabilities import ModelCapabilities
from forgex.schemas.agent import AgentRole
from forgex.schemas.plan import ArchitecturePlan
from forgex.schemas.task import Task
from forgex.sandbox.permissions import permission_set_for
from forgex.tools import ToolExecutor, build_tool_registry, create_context


class FakeRouter:
    def __init__(self, reply):
        self.reply = reply
        self.calls = []

    def capabilities_for(self, agent):
        return ModelCapabilities(model="fake", supports_tools=True)

    async def generate(self, agent, *, messages, system=None, **kw):
        self.calls.append({"messages": messages})
        return ModelResponse(content=self.reply, model="fake", provider="fake")

    async def aclose(self):
        return None


def write(tmp_path: Path, rel: str, content: str) -> None:
    path = tmp_path / rel
    path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    path.write_text(content, encoding="utf-8")


def make_tools(tmp_path, role):
    ctx = create_context(workspace=tmp_path, config=load_config(), agent=role)
    return ToolExecutor(registry=build_tool_registry(),
                        permissions=permission_set_for(role), context=ctx)


async def test_builder_instruction_includes_importer(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/helpers.py", "def tool():\n    return 42\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/api.py", "from .helpers import tool\n\nclass Api:\n    pass\n")
    router = FakeRouter(json.dumps({"status": "completed", "summary": "ok",
                                    "changed_files": ["src/helpers.py"]}))
    agent = BuilderAgent(router=router, tools=make_tools(tmp_path, AgentRole.BUILDER))
    task = Task(id="TASK-001", description="extend helpers tool",
                files=["src/helpers.py"])
    await agent.execute(task, plan=ArchitecturePlan(goal="demo"))

    instruction = router.calls[0]["messages"][0].content
    assert "src/helpers.py" in instruction          # planned seed
    assert "class Api" in instruction               # importer pulled in (impact context)


async def test_expert_coder_review_includes_importers(tmp_path):
    write(tmp_path, "src/helpers.py", "def tool():\n    return 43\n")
    write(tmp_path, "src/api.py", "from .helpers import tool\n\nclass Api:\n    pass\n")
    router = FakeRouter(json.dumps({"status": "completed", "summary": "clean",
                                    "changed_files": []}))
    agent = ExpertCoderAgent(router=router,
                             tools=make_tools(tmp_path, AgentRole.EXPERT_CODER))
    await agent.review(diff="+def tool():", changed_files=["src/helpers.py"])

    instruction = router.calls[0]["messages"][0].content
    assert "class Api" in instruction               # downstream impact visible to reviewer

PHASE 11 REPORT
PHASE COMPLETED: Phase 11 — Context Intelligence (pending your verification)

IMPLEMENTED:

Indexer (§17): cached project index (.forgex/cache/context-index.json) — files, language, size/mtime, symbols and imports; Python via stdlib AST (classes/functions/methods, Import/ImportFrom incl. relative), JS/TS via labeled regex heuristics; per-file size+mtime invalidation, pruning, parsed/reused/pruned stats; secret files and noise dirs never indexed; broken files tolerated
Dependency graph: import resolution (Python relative+absolute with shortest-path ambiguity rule; JS/TS relative specifiers with extension/index candidates; externals ignored), imports_of / importers_of with bounded BFS
Retriever (§16): query-term extraction (snake/camel splitting, stopwords), multi-signal scoring (seed/importer/importee/test-relation/recent/symbol/path) with auditable per-point reasons
Chunker (§16): AST-based Python chunks (module-head + def/class blocks with line markers), generic line windows, budget-aware selection with explicit elision markers — imports always survive
ContextManager: cache lifecycle, relevant_files, importers_of, compact symbol_map, build_context (budget + per-file cap + exclude) → ContextBundle.render()
Agent wiring (fail-open): Builder prefetch = planned files + related context; Expert Coder prefetch = changed files + their importers (impact analysis); Architect brief + repo symbol map
CLI: forgex context stats [--refresh], forgex context search --query ... [--seeds ...]

TESTS RUN: Not executed in this session. All offline (AST/regex extraction, resolution, scoring, budget enforcement, cache invalidation, and two agent-integration tests proving importer files reach the Builder/Reviewer prompts).

TEST RESULTS: ~24 new tests provided; suite total ≈ 291. Awaiting your pytest -q.

KNOWN ISSUES:

JS/TS symbol/import extraction is regex-based (labeled best-effort); Go/Rust/Java imports are not parsed yet — those files index (path/size/language) but don't join the dependency graph.
Absolute-Python-import resolution is a path heuristic; ambiguous names (two utils.py) resolve shortest-path — recorded behavior, not hidden.
No vector/semantic search (per §17, deliberately not required for MVP) — purely lexical + structural signals.
Whole-tree walk on every ensure_index() (parse is cached, walk is not) — fine to ~10k files; a file-count cap or mtime-based walk cache is a Phase 13 item.
Chunk elision markers can split syntax mid-block — models cope, but chunk boundaries are structural only for Python.
Context integrations add tokens to Builder/Coder prompts (bounded by budget caps) — the trade is fewer blind tool round-trips.
forgex context requires an initialized project (cache path lives in .forgex/cache/).

phase 12 

Thought Process
Phase 12 — the ensemble. Your dual-provider setup (Groq + OpenAI) is exactly what this phase is for: when enabled, two different models independently review the same diff in parallel — read-only, on explicit bindings with no fallback (ensemble diversity must be real) — findings are synthesized deterministically (corroboration counting = the "judge"), and the senior Expert Coder applies the justified fixes. Output-level collaboration only, exactly as §15 demands — no pretending weights are merged.

PHASE 12 — MULTI-MODEL ENSEMBLE
12.0 Design decisions (ADR additions)
Reviewers are read-only critics; one writer applies. Parallel reviewers with write access would clobber each other's edits. So each reviewer gets a READ/GIT/TEST permission set (the executor enforces no writes) and reports ReviewFindings; the standard Expert Coder (write-enabled, your expert_coder binding) receives the synthesized findings and applies/refutes them. This also matches §15's diagram: reviews → judge → combined findings → action.
Explicit bindings, no fallback. New ModelRouter.generate_with(provider=, model=) generates against a specific binding — and deliberately does not fall back (silently falling back would duplicate one reviewer's opinion and fake the ensemble). Reviewer resolution: ensemble.bindings if set, else expert_coder binding + fallback providers' default models (→ groq/llama-3.3-70b-versatile + openai_compatible/gpt-4o-mini on your config).
The judge is deterministic code, not an LLM. synthesize() clusters findings by lexical similarity (Jaccard ≥ 0.4 on content tokens); clusters raised by ≥2 distinct reviewers become confirmed (corroborated). Verdict: needs_changes if any confirmed major/critical finding, or a strict majority of reviewers say so. Minor-only findings + minority verdicts → ok (findings still recorded for the Supervisor). Auditable, cheap, testable.
Cost discipline: ok verdict skips the apply pass entirely (minor findings are recorded, not chased). needs_changes pays for exactly one apply pass. Reviewer count is capped 2–4 (bounded parallelism, §55).
Resilience: reviewers run via asyncio.gather(return_exceptions=True); a failed reviewer is recorded and the ensemble continues with the rest; all-failed → controlled ProviderError; interrupts propagate (never swallowed by the gather).
Dispatch inside review_work (late import to avoid the import cycle): engine + forgex review CLI get ensemble behavior with zero changes when ensemble.enabled=true. <2 resolvable reviewers → logged fallback to single-model review.
New: ENSEMBLE_COMPLETED event (reviewers, failures, verdict, confirmed/unique counts), reviewer:v1 prompt, forgex models shows active ensemble reviewers.

class ReviewFinding(BaseModel):
    """One issue found by an ensemble reviewer (spec section 15)."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    issue: str
    severity: Literal["critical", "major", "minor"] = "major"
    file: str | None = None
    suggestion: str | None = None


class ReviewFindings(BaseModel):
    """One reviewer's read-only verdict on a diff."""

    model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid")

    verdict: Literal["ok", "needs_changes"]
    summary: str
    findings: list[ReviewFinding] = Field(default_factory=list)

src/forgex/prompts/reviewer.md ✚

You are a Reviewer — one of several independent experts examining the samechanges (FORGEX ensemble). You are READ-ONLY: you cannot modify files.Report findings; another agent applies fixes.

Inspect the diff, the changed files, the tests, the dependencies, and theplan. Cross-check claims against the actual source with your tools —do not judge from the diff alone.

Report only real problems. Each finding needs: severity (critical, major,minor), the file, and a concrete suggestion. Do not invent problems toseem useful — "ok" is a valid and respectable verdict. Be concise.

When finished, respond with ONLY:{"verdict": "ok" | "needs_changes", "summary": "...", "findings": [{"issue": "...", "severity": "critical"|"major"|"minor", "file": "...", "suggestion": "..."}]}

Router: explicit bindings
src/forgex/models/router.py ✏ — add these methods to ModelRouter (e.g., right after capabilities_for):

python

    # -- explicit-binding generation (ensemble reviewers; spec sections 14, 15) --

    def binding_for(self, provider: str, model: str) -> ModelBinding:
        cfg = self._config.providers.get(provider)
        if cfg is None:
            msg = f"Unknown provider '{provider}' — check [providers.{provider}] in config"
            raise ConfigError(msg)
        resolved_model = model or cfg.default_model
        if not resolved_model:
            msg = f"No model for explicit binding on provider '{provider}'"
            raise ConfigError(msg)
        return ModelBinding(
            agent="explicit", provider_name=provider, model=resolved_model,
            provider=self._provider(provider),
            capabilities=self._registry.lookup(resolved_model),
        )

    def capabilities_for_binding(self, provider: str, model: str) -> ModelCapabilities:
        return self.binding_for(provider, model).capabilities

    async def generate_with(
        self,
        *,
        provider: str,
        model: str,
        agent: str,
        messages: list[Message],
        system: str | None = None,
        tools: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
        tool_choice: str | None = None,
        response_format: dict[str, str] | None = None,
        temperature: float | None = None,
        max_tokens: int | None = None,
        run_id: str | None = None,
    ) -> ModelResponse:
        """Generate against an EXPLICIT provider/model binding.

        No fallback, unlike generate(): ensemble diversity must be real —
        silently falling back would duplicate one reviewer's opinion.
        Usage is recorded under the given agent label."""
        binding = self.binding_for(provider, model)
        request = self._build_request(
            binding, messages, system=system, tools=tools, tool_choice=tool_choice,
            response_format=response_format, temperature=temperature,
            max_tokens=max_tokens,
        )
        start = time.perf_counter()
        try:
            response = await binding.provider.generate(request)
        except ForgexError as exc:
            self._record(
                binding, run_id=run_id,
                latency_ms=(time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000,
                error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}",
                attempts=getattr(exc, "attempts", 1),
            )
            raise
        self._record(binding, run_id=run_id,
                     latency_ms=(time.perf_counter() - start) * 1000,
                     response=response)
        return response
Expert coder: scope extraction + dispatch
src/forgex/agents/expert_coder.py ✏ — replace the entire review_work function (the last function in the file) with these three blocks. _single_review is the old review_work body, unchanged in behavior; the scope logic moves into resolve_review_scope (shared with the ensemble):

python

def resolve_review_scope(*, stores, task_id, base_commit):
    """Validate + resolve the review scope. Returns (task, diff, changed)
    or None when there is nothing to review. Task ids are validated BEFORE
    the nothing-to-review check, so unknown tasks always error."""
    task = None
    if task_id is not None:
        tasks = stores.tasks.load()
        task = next((t for t in tasks if t.id == task_id), None)
        if task is None:
            raise ProjectError(f"Unknown task: {task_id}")

    if base_commit is None and task_id is not None and not is_dirty(stores.root):
        record = find_pre_task_checkpoint(task_id, stores=stores)
        if record is not None and record.git_commit:
            base_commit = record.git_commit

    if base_commit:
        diff = git_diff_range(stores.root, base_commit)
        changed = git_changed_files(stores.root, base_commit)
    else:
        diff = git_diff(stores.root)
        changed = git_changed_files(stores.root)

    if not diff and not changed:
        return None
    return task, diff, changed


async def _single_review(*, stores, config, task_id=None, base_commit=None,
                         coder=None, approval=None) -> ExpertCoderResult | None:
    state = stores.state.load()
    if state is None:
        raise ProjectError("Project state missing — run `forgex init` first.")
    scope = resolve_review_scope(stores=stores, task_id=task_id, base_commit=base_commit)
    if scope is None:
        return None
    task, diff, changed = scope

    create_checkpoint(
        f"pre-review{'-' + task_id if task_id else ''}",
        stores=stores, description="before expert coder review",
    )
    agent = coder or build_coder(config=config, workspace=stores.root, stores=stores,
                                 approval=approval, run_id=state.current_run_id)
    owns_agent = coder is None
    try:
        result = await agent.review(task=task, plan=state.architecture_plan,
                                    research=state.research, diff=diff,
                                    changed_files=changed, errors=state.errors[-5:])
    except ForgexError as exc:
        state.errors.append(ErrorRecord(
            source="expert_coder", kind=type(exc).__name__,
            message=str(exc), run_id=state.current_run_id,
        ))
        stores.save_state(state)
        raise
    finally:
        if owns_agent:
            await agent.aclose()
    if result.changed_files:
        state.changed_files = sorted(set(state.changed_files) | set(result.changed_files))
        stores.save_state(state)
    return result


async def review_work(*, stores, config, task_id=None, base_commit=None,
                      coder=None, approval=None) -> ExpertCoderResult | None:
    """Review recent changes — single model, or the multi-model ensemble
    when config.ensemble.enabled (spec section 15)."""
    if config.ensemble.enabled:
        from forgex.agents.ensemble import ensemble_review  # late: avoids import cycle

        return await ensemble_review(
            stores=stores, config=config, task_id=task_id,
            base_commit=base_commit, applier=coder, approval=approval,
        )
    return await _single_review(stores=stores, config=config, task_id=task_id,
                                base_commit=base_commit, coder=coder,
                                approval=approval)
The ensemble
src/forgex/agents/ensemble.py ✚

python

"""Multi-model ensemble review (spec section 15).

Output-level collaboration ONLY (per spec — no weight merging). N
independent reviewer models examine the same diff in PARALLEL, read-only;
findings are synthesized deterministically (corroboration counting = the
judge); the senior Expert Coder applies the justified fixes. Reviewers run
on explicit provider/model bindings with NO fallback — ensemble diversity
must be real.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
import re
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Literal

from pydantic import BaseModel, Field

from forgex.agents.base import BaseAgent, prefetch_files
from forgex.agents.expert_coder import _single_review, build_coder, resolve_review_scope

def make_config(**ensemble) -> ForgexConfig:
    return ForgexConfig(
        models=__import__("forgex.config.schema", fromlist=["ModelsConfig"]).ModelsConfig(
            expert_coder=AgentModelBinding(provider="groq", model="llama-3.3-70b-versatile"),

Thought Process
Phase 13 — the attack phase. I red-team everything we've built per §75, ship the fixes the audit finds, and write the honest security documentation. Spoiler from the audit: I found one real policy bypass (env rm, xargs rm, find -exec execute denied commands as arguments), one real leak vector (secrets in non-standard filenames flow into model context — only .env-style names were guarded), one missing budget (nothing capped total tool-loop transcript size), and one missing guardrail (two concurrent forgex build runs can corrupt state — no run lock). All fixed below, plus crash-proofing and the documented residual limitations.

PHASE 13 — PRODUCTION HARDENING
13.0 Audit findings → fixes
#
§75 threat
Finding
Fix
F1	Path traversal	NUL-byte / undecodable paths crash as ValueError instead of a controlled ToolError	_resolve_path catches ValueError/OSError
F2	Command injection	Real bypass: env rm, xargs rm, find -exec rm, nohup/timeout/nice/stdbuf/watch wrappers, tee (arbitrary file writes) defeat the deny list via argv[0]	Launchers + tee added to DEFAULT_DENIED
F3	Secret leakage	Secrets in non-standard files (settings.py with api_key = "gsk_...") reach model context via read/search/prefetch/context-manager	New redact_patterns() (known key shapes + generic key=value literals + PEM blocks) applied at every content-on-ramp
F4	Symlink attacks	Writing through a final-component symlink is ambiguous (writes the target, surprises the agent); trivial TOCTOU remains	_assert_mutable refuses final-component symlinks; TOCTOU window documented
F5	Oversized prompts	Per-tool caps existed, but nothing bounded total transcript size — a looping agent could blow any context window	Transcript budget in the tool loop: soft "wrap up" notice at 75%, hard AgentOutputError at 150k chars
F6	Race conditions	Two concurrent forgex build processes corrupt state	Run lock (.forgex/run.lock, PID+host+time, POSIX liveness check, stale takeover, released on any Python-level exit)
F7	Failure recovery	A non-ForgexError crash in the Builder left the task in_progress forever and killed the run dirty	execute_task + engine task-loop catch Exception (never KeyboardInterrupt — interrupts must preserve in_progress for resume) → task marked failed, error recorded, run continues

Also: git argv-injection proven inert (tampered git_commit is one argv element → invalid revision → clean GitError), docker policy gating verified, prompt-injection defense verified structurally (repo content only inside tagged user messages, never in system prompts).

src/forgex/utils/redact.py ✏ — append (keep redact as-is):

python

import re

# Known secret shapes + generic key/value literals + PEM blocks. Pattern-based,
# best-effort (spec section 46): over-redaction is safe; under-redaction is not.
_SECRET_PATTERNS: tuple[re.Pattern[str], ...] = (
    re.compile(r"gsk_[A-Za-z0-9]{20,}"),
    re.compile(r"sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}"),
    re.compile(r"AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16}"),
    re.compile(r"ghp_[A-Za-z0-9]{30,}"),
    re.compile(r"github_pat_[A-Za-z0-9_]{20,}"),
    re.compile(r"glpat-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,}"),
    re.compile(r"xox[baprs]-[A-Za-z0-9-]{10,}"),
    re.compile(r"-----BEGIN [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----[\s\S]*?-----END [A-Z ]*PRIVATE KEY-----"),
    re.compile(
        r"(?i)\b(api[_-]?key|secret|token|password|passwd)\b['\"]?\s*[:=]\s*['\"][^'\"\n]{8,}['\"]"
    ),
)


def redact_patterns(text: str) -> str:
    """Scrub secret-looking content before it enters model context (§46).
    Applied at every content-on-ramp: read_file, search, prefetch, context."""
    for pattern in _SECRET_PATTERNS:
        text = pattern.sub("***REDACTED***", text)
    return text
src/forgex/tools/filesystem.py ✏ — three edits:

python

# 1) imports: add
from forgex.utils.redact import redact_patterns

# 2) replace _resolve_path (F1) and _assert_mutable (F4):
def _resolve_path(ctx: ToolContext, raw: str) -> Path:
    try:
        return resolve_in_workspace(ctx.workspace, raw)
    except PathViolationError as exc:
        raise ToolError(str(exc)) from exc
    except (ValueError, OSError) as exc:  # null bytes, undecodable paths, FS errors
        raise ToolError(f"invalid path: {exc}") from exc


def _assert_mutable(ctx: ToolContext, path: Path, raw: str) -> None:
    if path == ctx.workspace:
        raise ToolError("refusing to modify the workspace root")
    # F4: refuse writing/deleting THROUGH a final-component symlink — the
    # operation would hit the target file, not the link (ambiguity + a
    # swapped-link TOCTOU vector). A resolve-time race window remains —
    # documented in docs/security.md; the sandbox is the real boundary.
    lexical = Path(raw)
    if not lexical.is_absolute():
        lexical = ctx.workspace / lexical
    if lexical.is_symlink():
        raise ToolError(f"refusing to modify through a symlink: {raw}")
    rel = Path(_rel(ctx, path))
    top = rel.parts[0] if rel.parts else ""
    if top in {".forgex", ".git"}:
        raise ToolError("refusing to modify protected path (.forgex/ and .git/ are system-owned)")

# 3) update the four call sites to pass raw:
#    _assert_mutable(ctx, path, raw)   # in _write_file, _create_file, _delete_file, _move_file

# 4) in _read_file, redact before line-capping (F3):
    text = redact_patterns(raw_bytes[:MAX_READ_BYTES].decode("utf-8", errors="replace"))

src/forgex/orchestration/lockfile.py ✚

python

"""Single-active-run lock (production hardening, spec section 45).

Advisory lock file .forgex/run.lock {pid, host, created_at}. A live lock
(POSIX liveness check via os.kill(pid, 0)) refuses to start a second run.
Stale locks (dead pid, or older than STALE_AFTER_SECONDS where liveness
cannot be checked) are taken over. The lock is released on every
Python-level exit; only a hard kill leaves it behind — stale takeover
recovers. Existing projects: add "run.lock" to .forgex/.gitignore.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import socket
import time
from pathlib import Path

from forgex.schemas.error import ProjectError

LOCK_NAME = "run.lock"
STALE_AFTER_SECONDS = 24 * 3600


def _pid_alive(pid: int) -> bool:
    if pid <= 0:
        return False
    if os.name == "posix":
        try:
            os.kill(pid, 0)
            return True
        except ProcessLookupError:
            return False
        except PermissionError:
            return True  # exists, owned by another user
    return False  # cannot check on this platform — staleness rules apply


class RunLock:
    def __init__(self, path: Path) -> None:
        self._path = Path(path)
        self._acquired = False

    def acquire(self) -> None:
        if self._path.exists():
            data = None
            try:
                data = json.loads(self._path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
            except (OSError, ValueError):
                pass
            if isinstance(data, dict) and "pid" in data:
                pid = int(data.get("pid", 0))
                age = time.time() - float(data.get("created_at", 0))
                if _pid_alive(pid):
                    raise ProjectError(
                        f"another FORGEX run appears active (pid {pid}, "
                        f"host {data.get('host', '?')}): {self._path}")
                if os.name != "posix" and age < STALE_AFTER_SECONDS:
                    raise ProjectError(
                        f"another FORGEX run may be active (pid {pid}) and liveness "
                        f"cannot be verified on this platform — remove {self._path} "
                        f"if you are sure it is stale")
        self._path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        self._path.write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": os.getpid(), "host": socket.gethostname(),
            "created_at": time.time(),
        }), encoding="utf-8")
        self._acquired = True

    def release(self) -> None:
        if self._acquired:
            try:
                self._path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
            except OSError:
                pass
            self._acquired = False

docs/security.md ✚
Security
FORGEX executes AI-generated code and commands on your machine. This documentis the honest security model — mitigations AND residual risks (spec sections19, 20, 45, 46, 47, 75).

The defense stack
Workspace confinement — every agent path goes throughresolve_in_workspace: .. traversal, absolute escapes, and symlinkescapes are rejected. .forgex/ and .git/ are system-owned; theworkspace root is untouchable.
Command policy (deny list incl. all shells + argument launchers,optional allowlist, network-command approval, auto/ask/safe modes) →approval gate → sandbox (Docker: no host FS outside the workspacemount, --network none by default, dropped capabilities, read-onlyrootfs, uid-mapped).
Argv-only execution — commands are never interpreted by a shell;shell injection is structurally impossible. Git operations (includingrollback) are argv-based: forged commit values fail as invalid revisions.
Secret hygiene — keys live only in env vars; the child environmentis a sanitized allowlist; .env-style files are unreadable by agents;all content entering model context is pattern-scrubbed(redact_patterns); provider error text is redacted.
Untrusted data discipline (§47) — repository content only everenters prompts inside tagged blocks in user messages; system promptsare static. Instructions inside repo content are data, and agents haveno permission to act on most of what an injection might ask for(permissions are enforced per tool call, not per prompt).
Deterministic gates — no model verdict can pass a red test suite,and no run completes with unfinished tasks (quality gates override LLMjudgment).
Recoverability — checkpoints before every mutation; rollback alwayscreates a safety checkpoint first; interrupts save state; one active runper project (run lock).
Residual limitations (known, accepted)
Without the Docker sandbox, a locally executed process (e.g.python -c ...) can read the host filesystem and write to absolute pathsoutside the workspace — the policy layer gates commands, not behavior.Enable sandbox.enabled for behavioral containment. permissions.mode = "auto" additionally auto-approves network commands — use ask (default)or safe on untrusted projects.
Secret redaction is pattern-based. Non-obvious secret formats(high-entropy strings without key-shaped names) can pass through. Neverpoint FORGEX at directories holding real production secrets.
A TOCTOU window remains between path resolution and file mutation(final-component symlinks are refused; a swapped parent link racedbetween check and write is not fully prevented without O_NOFOLLOWsemantics).
Prompt-injection defense is structural, not semantic — a model canstill be convinced by repo content; what it cannot do is exceed itspermission set, escape the workspace, or pass the quality gates.
Sandbox image trust: the configured image is trusted code; a maliciousimage would run with the workspace mounted. Pin images you control.
Windows: run-lock liveness cannot be verified (staleness rules apply);sandbox uid mapping relies on Docker Desktop.
Verification commands run with the sanitized environment but the project'sown dependencies — a malicious project can execute code in conftest.pyat test time. Run untrusted projects with the sandbox enabled.
Tests

# -- path traversal battery (§75) ---------------------------------------------

@pytest.mark.parametrize("raw", ["../x", "a/../..", "a/b/../../../c", "./..", "x/../../y"])
def test_traversal_battery_rejected(tmp_path, raw):
    with pytest.raises(PathViolationError):
        resolve_in_workspace(tmp_path, raw)


def test_redact_patterns_scrubs_known_shapes():
    text = ("gsk_abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwx sk-abcdefghijklmnopqrstuv12 "
            "AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE ghp_" + "a" * 35 + " "
            'api_key = "supersecretvalue123\" '
            "-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----\nabc\n-----END RSA PRIVATE KEY----- "
            "safe_string stays")
    scrubbed = redact_patterns(text)
    for leaked in ("gsk_abc", "sk-abc", "AKIAIOSFODNN7EXAMPLE", "supersecretvalue", "BEGIN RSA"):
        assert leaked not in scrubbed
    assert "safe_string stays" in scrubbed
    assert scrubbed.count("***REDACTED***") >= 4


async def run_tool(tmp_path, name, arguments, *, role=AgentRole.BUILDER):
    ctx = create_context(workspace=tmp_path, config=load_config(), agent=role)
    executor = ToolExecutor(registry=build_tool_registry(),
               for call in router.calls for m in call["messages"])

import json

import time
from pathlib import Path

import pytest

from tests.unit.test_engine import PLAN, FakeBuilder, FakeCoder, FakePlanner, FakeSupervisor, run_engine, seed

from forgex.config import load_config
